Only Data-class kinds are registered (voice/pager never); frequency band per kind. Client: web/onair-web/onair-decoders.js (tables, small map, FT8 list, picture viewers). Tests: tests/server/test_web_decoders.cpp (encoders, PNG round trip, strips, event size limits, every decoder on noise through the hub, ADS-B end to end) and node tests in tests/web/run.js (ctest web_decoders, web_client). Not tried with a real radio or browser. No NOAA APT (no decoder in the program). Possible merge conflicts: web_hub.h/.cpp (snapshot hook), CMakeLists.txt (ONAIR_WEB_SOURCES append, test block), server/main.cpp (registerWebDecoders call).
## 2026-10-23 OnAir-channels: why submissions were refused (channels-fix-030)
Causes of the 'invalid' issues: (1) DAB national multiplexes: the validator limited the NUMBER of service names to 80, a multiplex carries 80-110 and the app repeated names and sent '?' placeholders (#23 #58 #68 #71 #100 #130): validator now counts each name once (limit 200), app (issueBody) dedupes, drops '?' and caps at 150. (2) dab-tii (#26 #63 #185, #120 report): the app opened the GitHub form with empty lat/lon and the validator refused the row; edits on the form were lost. App now asks for latitude/longitude (decimal comma accepted) and site in a dialog first, needs a chosen country; the data repo re-checks an issue when it is edited. (3) control characters (#11 #18 #32): already fixed by text::clean, pass now. (4) #124 never processed, #77 and #120 are free text. Repo work is on branches fixes-2026-10 and fixes-2026-10-br165-after-79 of adswill/OnAir-channels (not merged).

Real: 474 MHz T2 32K 1/8 and 554 MHz DTMB PN945 sure. See docs/AUTODETECT.md for limits (no real voice, DVB-S/C, ISDB-T, ATSC recordings).

## 2026-10-23 Shared sample path performance (perf2-core-030)
Portable FFT (Linux/Windows): tiled branch-free bit reversal fused with stage 1, then 3 stages per pass (NEON, AVX2+FMA with each compiler's own contraction, plain C++ for SSE2): 2-2.8x on arm64, same bits (new tests/dsp/test_fft_bits.cpp, checked with clang/GCC arm64, clang x86 baseline and AVX2). RationalResampler: split path writes outputs in order after all phases (macOS -16%, same bits); NEON interleaved path on Linux arm64 (-38..-51%, same bits; not on macOS: vDSP_desamp order depends on the output count). File and BLUE readers: one loop per format (dsp/sample_convert.h). Scheduling: ThreadPriority::Decode for the shared pool (P-cores on Apple, big cores pinned on big.LITTLE Linux with the pool sized to them, EcoQoS for Background on Windows). CPU meter: common/cpu_meter.h + Engine::cpuPercent()/cpuUsage(), pool jobs charged to the caller's account; dect2cli prints it. 8 MHz DVB-T at 10 Msps on the Mac: about 0.9-1.0 core, the shared parts about 15-17% of it (resamplers most; spectrum 1.6%); decoder items (Viterbi 37%, per-carrier sincos 5%, two resampling stages) handed to perf2-dvbt-030. Details: docs/PERFORMANCE-CORE.md; bench: tools/bench/bench_core.sh.

## 2026-10-23 USRP transmit (fix-usrp-tx-030)
The native USRP driver had no TX path at all (RX only, makeNativeTxSink knew only the Pluto), so every USRP entry was classed receive-only. Added UsrpTx (UHD C API tx_streamer, fc32/sc16, TX/RX connector, B210 outputs 0/1, end-of-burst on close), optional TX symbols in UhdApi (hasTx), canTransmit set from hasTx, and DeviceInfo::txName so the TX list shows one "USRP B210 (serial)" instead of one entry per RX input. txUnavailableReason now names the radio once ("RTL-SDR dongles can only receive", "USRP B210 ... cannot transmit in this build: ..."); the TX window hides receive-only radios behind a count with a show toggle. docs/DEVICES.md has the table of transmit-capable radios. Unit tests in test_tx_polish (no hardware). Not verified on a real USRP.

## 2026-10-23 DVB-T / T2 / C decoding: lower thresholds, mobile, strong echoes (dec-dvbt-030)
DVB-T: scattered pilots averaged over time (gain from the measured noise and channel change), delay window narrowed to the measured spread,
symbols decoded 8 late with a cubic through each grid point's pilots (time interpolation), acquisition peak tested against the median, FFT
window from the rise of the CP correlation under a flat top (0 dB echo), the delay window follows the phase ramp (it folded paths after the
window moved). sens_dvb (C/N in band): 2K QPSK 1/2 4.5 -> 3.5, 16-QAM 1/2 10.0 -> 9.0, 64-QAM 2/3 2K/8K 17.5 -> 16.0, 8K 64-QAM 3/4 GI 1/32
19.0 -> 17.0, TU6 static 21 -> 19, +50 kHz +30 ppm 12.5 -> 10.5 dB; TU6 Doppler limit 2K 40 -> 100 Hz, 8K up to 30 Hz; 0 dB echo at 0.9 G:
never decoded -> clean from 18 dB. T2: the frame's channel cut into pieces under Doppler (5 -> 20 Hz at 20 dB), delaySpan noise floor from
the search range (L1 cells 3 dB cleaner at 4 dB), L1 sum-product fallback on weak signals: QPSK 1/2 5.0 -> 3.5-4.0 dB. Shared harness (quick):
dvbt awgn 17 -> 16, tu6 23 -> 18; dvbt2 tu6 none -> 20 dB. Checked against EN 300 744 Table A.1 and TS 102 831 Tables 44/46 (docs/SENSITIVITY.md).
Left: T2 data path ~1.5 dB from TS 102 831 on QPSK, DVB-T 8K above 30 Hz Doppler, DVB-C ~1 dB from theory (64-QAM 24 dB Es/N0, lock 0.16 s).
## 2026-10-23 DAB MP2 garble report (dec-dab-030)
User recording (11B, UEP services, 0.2.9): MP2 at 24 kHz (frame = two logical frames) was decoded per 24 ms logical frame: half frames, garbage plus concealment, 1.5x audio. MP2 frames are now cut out of the byte stream by their headers. New test dab_uep: all 64 UEP profiles, EEP A/B, mono/stereo, 48/24 kHz to the bit through the whole receiver. Conditional access (FIG 0/2 CAId, CA flag) read: scrambled stations not played, panel says so. NaN/Inf input samples zeroed (dab_realworld failed on Linux x86). Generator: 24 kHz and mono MP2, CA flag. Inferred from the decoded audio only (no IQ).

## 2026-10-23 Spectrum labels line up (fix-labels-230-030, #230)
Root cause: the waterfall reserves room for its Waterfall button and autolabel_logic.h used that narrowed pixel range as the whole axis, so waterfall labels sat on a stretched scale; boxes were also clamped to the plot edge, so they stuck while panning. New: View.ax0/ax1 = whole plot rectangle for the frequency-to-pixel map (xOf, same in both panes), px0/px1 only limit where boxes go. A name with its frequency in view is centred on it and clipped by the plot; a name with a nominal band (Label.bwMhz, from the band plan) stays while the band overlaps the view, box moved inside, stub arrow at the edge, no pointer. Yellow left stripe removed; a small triangle under the name points at the exact frequency; a thin bar on its own lane (2 lanes, no overlaps) shows the nominal band. Tests in tests/app/test_autolabel.cpp. Not verified on the reporters Mac.

- tx_studio: the test asked for CW at 48 kHz, below CW's 0.25 Msps minimum that makeTxGenerator enforces (it never passed on tx-studio-030 either); the test now uses 0.25 Msps and writes WAV from the same samples labelled 48 kHz.

NFC: the carrier at the IF centre is the reader's field (DC excepted in the test); hard 100 % ASK pauses gave -30 dBc far power, now raised-cosine edges (1 us down, 1.5 us up) inside ISO 14443-2. Speed: Remote ID fell below real time because the integration kept tx-opt-a's ble_gen.cpp whole; now merged with tx-opt-b's vector queue and FastSinCos. NR / Remote ID were only 1.6-1.9 x even on tx-opt-b, so the speed is now the median block (robust to a shared runner) and tx_quality_b / tx_quality_a_rt carry the timing label.
- tx: nfc was missing from the test's payload-kind table (mode-nfc added the mode, not the row).
- Golden digests re-recorded, each attributed by reverting only that change: ts_proto_rs (stream type 0x14 = DRA, dtmb-dra-030), pager_proto_rs (FLEX check nibble / long address, flexnext-030 "wip" commit), mesh_proto_rs (APRS message number stops at '}', fix-packet-030; macOS and portable digests).
- datasets: the dab-tii-XX pack ids had capitals (invalid dataset ids), now lower case; the "airports" pack repeated the bundled airports id, now one row with a note.
- swarm: PairResponder::offer("") was accepted (normalise("") == ""), now refused.
## 2026-10-23 Corrected LDPC for ATSC 3.0, ATSC 1.0 pilot loop, ISDB-T / DTMB measured (dec-satTv-030, part 2)
Two-minimum correction shared in dect2/dsp/minsum_corr.h. ATSC 3.0: Type B codes (6/15+, DVB-S2 structure, identical code words) on dect2::LdpcCode with the correction (0.82), data and L1; Type A on the general decoder with the correction (0.80; 16200 2/15: 0.92); ideal channel 0.2-0.4 dB gain (16200 8/15 at 1.4 dB: 2/40 failed words vs 37/40 for the old 8-bit OMS + float fallback; 64800 3/15 at -4.26 dB 1/20 vs 20/20). DTMB: the correction gained ~0.1 dB on an ideal channel but broke the CW interference case of test_dtmb_faults, so the chain stays plain min-sum (option kept in the decoder and Metal QC kernel). 8-bit decoder normalisation capped at 26/32. ATSC 1.0: pilot loop 0.0035 -> 0.0015 after ~0.1 s of steady pilot: AWGN 14.5 dB 28.7 % -> 0.4 % bad packets (bench_atsc; ~0.6 dB, within ~0.1 dB of the decoder alone); Wiener phase noise 5 Hz at 15.5 dB 63 % -> 6 %; 20 Hz still fails (open). ISDB-T measured (mode 3, GI 1/8, TI 1, 13 segments, 99 % packets): QPSK 1/2 4.15 dB, 2/3 5.85, DQPSK 1/2 5.95 vs ARIB 4.9 / 6.6 / 6.2: no change made. DVB-S2 lock (first packet, near threshold): 0.55 s at 1 MBd, 1.0 s at 333 kBd, 1.75 s at 125 kBd (hunt needs ~131k symbols; a 65k first try did not help, reverted). EN 302 307-1 Table 13 and -2 Tables 20a/20c checked against the code (all rows identical).
## 2026-10-23 Digital voice and pager sensitivity (dec-voice-030)
Measured with each mode's own test signal (makeModeSynth, 8-bit rounding, 20 s; blocks decoded vs SNR) with an A/B switch during the work.
- dPMR: low-pass symbol filter (1500 Hz) instead of the half-symbol box, ML soft Hamming (12,8), HI0+HI1 soft combining: 50 % point 14 -> 7 dB, 90 % >14 -> 9 dB.
- NXDN: low-pass symbol filter (0.625 x baud), LICH single-bit soft repair: 50 % 10 -> 5 dB, 90 % 12 -> 7 dB. A separate +-4 kHz NXDN48 channel filter gave 2-3 dB more but lost a status message in test_nxdn_rx's off-centre +150 ppm case: reverted, a follow-up.
- P25: channel +-6 kHz (was 8.5), symbol filter 3 kHz (was 3.6), ML soft Hamming (10,6) / Golay (18,6) before RS: 50 % 8 -> 6 dB, 90 % 9 -> 8 dB; +-3 kHz offsets fine.
- D-STAR: channel +-4.5 kHz (was 7): 50 % 9 -> 7 dB; a call also ends on a channel power drop (the narrower filter made post-carrier noise look like bits).
- POCSAG/FLEX: Chase soft repair (6 least reliable bits, at most 4 changed), addresses only from doubtful bits (FLEX address words found from the BIW), message words boldly; the first batch after a searched sync needs 10 of 16 hard-valid words (removes false 2400 bd pages seen even at 30 dB). 8 seeds x 27 pages: right at 0 dB 48 -> 76, at 2 dB 145 -> 160; false pages 66 -> 33 over 2..-3 dB; noise alone 0.
- Head-to-head, multimon-ng 1.6.2 built in scratch (same signal FM-demodulated rtl_fm-style, 6 seeds x 27 pages): 0 dB OnAir 56 vs 29, 2 dB 119 vs 62, 4 dB 150 vs 98; multimon-ng prints 6-20 false addresses per point, OnAir 0-2 (10 at -2 dB).
- New tests test_{dpmr,nxdn,p25,dstar,pager}_sens (tests/common/voice_sens.h) hold these numbers plus noise-only checks.
- Unchanged: DMR already ~4-5 dB (RRC + soft BPTC); M17's cliff at ~5 dB is the FM threshold (the acquisition gates were not it); Tetrapol integrates the differential precoding after a discriminator (one raw error flips the rest of the frame: needs coherent phase tracking); TETRA offline numbers are unreliable because the capture worker skips captures when fed faster than real time (about 6 dB for 50 %, against "about 20 dB" for sdrpp-tetra; not verified in real time).
- Known: test_pager_proto_rs digest mismatch on this Mac exists without these changes (pager_proto untouched). Nothing tested on real signals.

## 2026-10-23 DVB-T / T2 / C optimisation, second pass (perf2-dvbt-030)
Profiled with bench_dvb (stage CPU) and `sample`, every change compared old against new on the same input. Exact (bit for bit): Viterbi NEON step with two branch metrics instead of four and renormalisation every 16 steps (64.6 instructions per step), table depuncturing (3.8x), split-array FFT input, gathered equaliser, GridInterpolator keeps its delay phasors and convCorrShort runs 32 outputs at a time (-50%), T2 LDPC in blocks of 20 checks with the min-sum state in registers (-7% per iteration), T2 demapper by a minimum tree in NEON (-45%), cell de-interleaver without divisions, four phasor chains side by side, PLP cells straight into the stream, NEON ClockCorrector (1.9x), byte-table CRC-8 in the TS unpacker (T2 TS out -65%), buffers kept with their threads. Not bit-identical inside, TS digests unchanged: DVB-T one ExactResampler for rate change plus clock correction (36 taps instead of 32 + 32), T2 resampler kernel sized for the occupied band (32 instead of 64 taps). Dropped: DVB-C four partial sums (digest changed), int16 LDPC prototype (same FER, not faster). Result under the guard: DVB-T 8 MHz -40% (0.675 -> 0.404 CPU s/s), T2 32K 256-QAM -21%, DVB-C unchanged; whole engine (dect2cli, 8 MHz DVB-T at 10 Msps) 79% -> 55% of a core. Figures and limits in docs/PERFORMANCE-DVB.md. The laptop runner cannot build this ref yet: core/src/fm/fm_rx.cpp:364 fails with GCC 13 -Wchanges-meaning (from integ-30).

## 2026-10-23 DVB-T app CPU, 8-bit Viterbi, GPU Viterbi wiring (perf2-dvbt-030, round 3)
The whole app measured (renamed dect2 copy, --hidden --mute, the 8 MHz DVB-T recording at 10 Msps, 60 s, median of 3): integ-30 70.8% of a core with the default panels, 68.6% with the Log tab in front; this branch 49.6% / 47.6% (Arclamp: 89%). New: 8-bit two-run NEON Viterbi for DVB-T (BER equal to the exact decoder near the waterfall, tests/fec/test_viterbi8_ber.cpp), DVB-T resampler 14 lobes, one reciprocal in the equaliser, Engine::setSpectrumBlocksPerFrame, bench_dvb --gpu; GPU Viterbi for DVB-T wired (GPU mode only, experimental), Direct3D 11 backend opt-in (DECT2_GPU_VITERBI_D3D11=1, untested on Windows), tests/fec/test_viterbi_gpu.cpp. Numbers in docs/PERFORMANCE-DVB.md.

docs/FM-AUDIT.md. 2026-10-23 (dec-sat-030): satellite / positioning / cellular decoder pass, measured on the synthetic generators. GNSS: maximum-likelihood bit edge for weak LNAV signals (the 1 ms sign histogram fails under ~30 dB-Hz), LNAV lock indicator on 10 ms sums, falling FLL gain before the PLL hand-over for weak signals, a 20 ms coherent 10 Hz Costas loop once bit-synchronised under 30 dB-Hz; test_gnss_sweep over 3 seeds x 40/36/34 dB-Hz skies, 45 s: 48 -> 57 satellites with frames decoded, fixes 5 -> 5, the 40 dB-Hz positions identical. RINEX 3.04 observation + GPS navigation output (core/src/gnss/gnss_rinex.cpp, gnsstool --rinex, tests/gnss/test_gnss_rinex.cpp); an independent single-point solution from the two files is within ~20 m (no iono model). Meteor LRPT: narrower carrier loop and symbol clock after 0.5 s, half-symbol-late N2-x locks moved to the normal state on weak signals, immediate re-search after lost sync or 3 bad frames, SNR from moments; threshold QPSK 72k 4 -> 2-2.5 dB, OQPSK 72k 5 -> 3.5 dB, OQPSK 80k 6 -> 4 dB Es/N0, every existing case the same frame count. LTE: tests/cell/test_cell_snr (single cell found + MIB to -10 dB band SNR, SIB1 to -6 dB; not changed). HRPT: narrow carrier loop 0.002 (was 0.008), MetOp AHRPT threshold ~4 -> 2 dB Es/N0, existing cases unchanged. GNSS cold search: with the radio offset remembered the 64 ms search comes second (~10 s instead of ~45 s; zenith 32 dB-Hz sky 0 -> 4 satellites decoded in 60 s). Open: GNSS acquisition threshold ~30-31 dB-Hz (GNSS-SDR typical 35) and without a remembered offset the 64 ms search still waits ~45 s; smoothing the LRPT frequency hand-over broke the 80k interleaved mode at 14 dB and was dropped; nothing verified on real signals.

## 2026-10-23: spectrum-match-030 (owner: the earlier looks were "AI waterfalls"; tuning "super unsmooth")
Measured the two reference screenshots (palettes, floor colour, grain, trace/fill/band colours) and rebuilt the pipeline to match on real recordings (31 m AM, HF band, UHF mux): 2 FFT blocks a frame, FFT up to 64k, history as fine as the FFT, power-averaged rows and trace (time constant follows bins per pixel: no comb at big FFTs), levels per Hz, auto range on the view floor (Navy heat / Blue green palettes, each with its floor place), one look, neutral palette names. tools/spec_render renders the app pipeline offscreen to PNG. Tuning: one tuning thread per engine with a newest-wins mailbox (LatestSlot, test), engine start/stop/retune share a lock; the big readout shows the listening (VFO) frequency with centre / hardware named beside it (freq_model.h, test); readout edits move the VFO inside the band, the radio only past its edge. Waterfall scale under its button and draggable; crisp tuning-style icons. Pipeline CPU 4.4 % -> 1.6 % of a core at 20 Msps (harness). Details: docs/SPECTRUM-RESEARCH.md.

restoration, high-band envelope smoothing + low-fluctuation noise, harmonic high-band extension from the rectified excitation, comfort-noise

### 2026-10-24 Fastext buttons shared one ImGui ID (fix-imgui-ids-031)
Discord report: the red "Programmer error: 5 visible items with conflicting ID" box in Teletext on a page without Fastext links. Cause: the empty-state label format "-##fx%d" was given (link, i), so %d took link (0) and all five buttons were "-##fx0". Fixed in app/panels/tv.cpp. Users no longer get ImGui's conflict box (io.ConfigDebugHighlightIdConflicts is off unless DECT2_IDCHECK is set). Test hook: DECT2_IDCHECK=1 prints every duplicate ID per window per frame to stderr and a --shot run exits 3; DECT2_IDCHECK_TTX=1 draws the Teletext controls with no stream. The hook flagged the old code (1596 hits) and the fixed build is clean over the Teletext/TV tabs, the mode list ids, map, tour, credits, memory and touch sheet. Also fixed: --tab Teletext opened Recordings (stale sub-tab index).

## 2026-10-25: fix32-dtmb (3.2: #275 CDR next to loud FM, #319 SDRplay stop/quit, #254 DTMB choppy)
#275: the sample is on WeTransfer (not downloaded), so the cause was reproduced with the generator: a hybrid CDR beside a loud FM station. The channel centring (ChannelCentre) moves the strongest 100 kHz block of the band to the middle when the receiver has not locked after 1.5 s, and it stayed there for good: a loud neighbour (or a signal that takes longer than 1.5 s to lock) left the receiver on the wrong station for the whole recording. ChannelCentre::setRetry(): centre, strongest block (3 s), centre again for twice as long, next block, ... until locked; CDR uses it (other modes unchanged). Test tests/cdr/test_cdr_loudfm.cpp (fails without the fix: stuck at +600 kHz / -800 kHz, passes with it). Measured, not fixed: an FM station 400 kHz away that is 30-40 dB over the digital signal pulls the SNR from 26 to 16 / 7 dB (rectangular FFT window leakage); a station within 300 kHz overlaps the hybrid sidebands and cannot be handled. Not verified against the reporter's file.
#319: DTMB itself: new tests/dtmb/test_dtmb_longrun.cpp (radio overflows, impulse noise, zero dropouts, level steps, carrier, 15 min of signal) shows no stuck feed() (worst 0.2 s) and no memory growth, so the hang is not in the DTMB receiver. SDRplay driver: Stop Receiver joined the stream thread, which waits in sdrplay_api_Uninit(); with a stalled stream or service that never returns. Uninit now runs on a helper thread with a 4 s limit (then the radio stays selected and OnAir goes on, with a message), and sdrplay_api_Close() is no longer called at exit (the SIGABRT inside libsdrplay_api's free() on quit that a second reporter saw, even without a radio). Needs a user test with an RSP1 / the SDRplay API installed on Linux.
#254: not reproduced (no recording); the receiver keeps up with 10 Msps in the tests. Likely lost USB samples of the Airspy R2 at 10 Msps or a slow computer; asked for the log lines "dropped" and the CPU load.

## 2026-10-26: feat32-modes2 (ROS, SSDV, EBICab / Eurobalise)
Three receive modes approved from the Discord requests. Families 205 (ROS HF, engine standard 207, poll slot 182), 206 (SSDV, 208, 183) and 207 (EBICab / Eurobalise, 209, 184); kNumFamilies 208, kPollSeqSlots 185 (family 204 was already the Flipper chat, so the brief's 204-206 moved up by one; the Flipper row was added to docs/MODE-NUMBERS.md).
ROS: the existing identifier (core/src/ros) is now a mode (core/src/roshf): sideband audio, a waterfall, a measurement every 2 s on the last 16 s (then the last 8 s for a drifting radio), the sub-mode ROS 4 / 8 / 16 baud, the tone range marked on the waterfall, a log. Text stays empty: "partial, needs a public spec" (no open ROS decoder exists to port). Tolerates +-150 Hz tuning, 1 Hz/s drift, 300 ppm symbol clock, 3 dB SNR; 2 Hz/s drift is not detected.
SSDV: fsphil/ssdv (GPL-3.0-or-later, codeberg.org/fsphil/ssdv, GitHub copy is archived) vendored in third_party/ssdv with its notices (stderr diagnostics compiled out), stb_image for the JPEG view. The receiver slices the FM discriminator of the packet front end at 1200 - 19200 baud (plain) and 300 - 1200 baud (RTTY-framed bytes) at once, both polarities, finds 0x55 0x66 / 0x67, checks CRC-32 and Reed-Solomon, rebuilds the JPEG as packets arrive (grey gaps), saves JPEG files, PNG copy through the picture folder. Not handled: scrambled / AX.25 / IL2P framing, Wenet LDPC, Horus 4FSK, LoRa. Receiver tuning found with the generator: bit integration 0.8 bit and clock-loop pull 0.8 (0.5 / 0.7 lost packets at Eb/N0 16 dB).
EBICab: the brief says the balise up-link is 27.095 MHz FSK; the public sources (SUBSET-036) say 27.095 MHz is the train's AM tele-powering and the FSK up-link is 4.234 MHz (3.951 / 4.516 MHz, 564.48 kbit/s), so the mode receives that. SUBSET-036 is public and complete for the coding, so the Eurobalise telegrams (1023 / 341 bit) are decoded with the receiver rules of 4.3.4.1 and the SUBSET-026 header; EBICab 700 / 900 formats are not published and appear only as listed bursts (length hint 32 / 255 bits). "Needs a user test": two readings (scrambler output, header layout) are unconfirmed without a real telegram; the radio must reach 4.2 MHz at 4 Msps or more.
Tests: ctest -R "ssdv|ebicab|roshf" (proto, rx with impairments, engine, mode wiring). Not tested on air.

Radio settings > Import Icom firmware... plus a "?" help (Windows path C:\Windows\System32\IC-R8600\IC-R8600_usb_iq.spt); without the file the radio shows "needs Icom firmware, see ?". ## 2026-10-26: feat32-quality (decoder quality and lock time)
Harnesses first, then fixes only where a metric moved. New: tests/dmr|p25|nxdn/test_*_short.cpp (12 to 49 short calls of 0.3 to 1 s per fault: share decoded, frames lost, delay to the first voice burst; tests/common/short_burst.h), tests/dvbt/test_dvbt_lock.cpp (time to first good TS packet), lock-by-condition rows in tests/dab/test_dab_sens.cpp, tests/source/test_agc_burst.cpp (weak signal plus a strong transmitter that keys up). Measured and left alone: DMR first voice burst at 95 ms (the header burst before it is the floor), P25 and NXDN short calls decode from the first frame up to 4.5 kHz off, DVB-T first good packet 150 ms (8K) / 45 ms (2K), DAB sync 0.15 s and first audio 0.5 s (the 16-CIF time interleaver is the floor). Fixed: (1) DMR coarse carrier search followed the skirt of a strong neighbour on the next channel (a continuous carrier 12.5 kHz away pulled the oscillator off the wanted channel: 12% of short direct-mode calls decoded, now 100% to +10 dB and 98% at +20 dB); (2) P25 and NXDN had no frequency capture beyond the channel filter, so a carrier 5 kHz or more off decoded nothing (P25 0% at 5-6 kHz, NXDN 3% at 6 kHz, both 100% now; core/include/dect2/dsp/mean_afc.h, only past 4.7 kHz so nothing below it changes); (3) automatic gain: a transmitter that keys up every few seconds pushed the ceiling down about 4 dB per burst and the climb back stopped short for good on a coarse stage table (gain 32 dB below the start after six bursts, now 12 dB, changes per burst 3.8-7.2 down to 2.8-5.5). Known and left: +30/+40 dB neighbours (DMR 0%, P25 67%), the P25 mean-AFC can follow a +40 dB neighbour's leakage in the gaps, the DMR cold-start AFC needs 0.2-0.45 s for a carrier 7 kHz off. The friend's DVB-T recording is no longer on disk, so the real-signal check is synthetic only.

## 2026-10-27: issues #351 #350 #344 #342 (fix33-issues2)
- #351 APRS transmit 50 kHz low. Cause: most modes listen `tuneOffsetHz` above the dial (away from the radio's DC spike) and their generators put the signal that far below the radio's centre, but the transmitter told the radio the dial itself, so every such mode went out `tuneOffsetHz` low (packet 50 kHz; a survey of the generators showed 54 modes with the signal at -tuneOffset). `TxSession` now adds `txModeTuneOffsetHz(mode)` to the radio frequency (not for modes with their own transmit chain, not for Hamlib rigs which only key); the IQ file sink labels its centre with the radio's frequency; the TX panel shows the real "radio is told" value. Anyone who typed a manual offset to compensate must set it back to 0. Not done: the "Repeat" / "seconds" fields (a single frame is repeated back to back for the whole time; what the reporter expects is unclear).
- #350 RDS text with Chinese stations: text buffers keep bytes and become UTF-8 on output; 0x80..0xFF as EBU Latin, or GB2312 when the bytes pair up (auto); tested with synthetic groups.
- #344 text can be copied: hovering any ImGui text and Ctrl+C (or Ctrl+Insert) copies it, a right-click gives a "Copy" menu (patch in third_party/imgui TextEx).
- #342 the Auto gain choice is saved per receiver (`agcOn`), and each receiver remembers its main tab: selecting its chip again returns to it.
- #349 P25 with two radios: not built; see the design in the report (needs the follower's voice-radio action and an app side driving a second receiver).

AIS map: "labels" button (zoomed in / off / all; off still names the selected and hovered vessel), saved as aisLabels. ## 2026-10-27: fix33-tetrapol (decoder stops after a frequency change)
Cause: tuning in a narrow-channel mode resets the receiver (retuneReset). TETRAPOL reset() rebuilt its telemetry so the report number fell to 0, while the engine kept the last number it had taken and ignores reports numbered at or below it, so the screen froze for minutes until a stop and start cleared it (DMR numbers its reports from a counter that survives reset, which is why it was fine). Fix: TETRAPOL keeps a counter that only counts up and keeps its cells and log over the reset; the engine also zeroes its remembered report numbers after a reset (primary mode), the same as it already did for the second mode. New tests: test_tetrapol_engine (3 channel steps through the engine, carrier offset, must report and lock again), testRetune in test_tetrapol_rx (offset and centre changes, other cell, inverted, 2.4 Msps). The engine test fails without the fix. Other modes keep their counter across reset already, apart from the engine-side safety net now covering all.
## 2026-10-27: fix33-trunking (trunked radio audit, P25 Phase 1 and 2)
Audit of the P25 trunking layer, DMR/NXDN/TETRA included. Found by reading the code and by new impairment harnesses (tests/p25/test_p25_impair.cpp, tests/p25p2/test_p25p2_impair.cpp, the generator takes a TSBK script now). Fixed: (1) Phase 2 receiver locked only within about 700 Hz of the carrier (45 degrees a symbol at the differential slicer; an RTL-SDR at 850 MHz is 800 Hz per ppm): it now searches the sync at +-2.4 kHz and recentres the front end, same code path as Phase 1's AFC; before/after 1 kHz and 2 kHz offsets: no lock / 100 % of the voice frames, noise alone gives no false sync. (2) #348: the follower searched the neighbours' channels (placed with this site's identifier table: 857.4875 MHz on a 770 MHz system), waited 3 s per candidate (a retune restarts the receiver, which needs longer) and retuned to the frequency it was on; own channels only, 12 s dwell, no retune onto the current frequency. Scenario with a 4 s re-acquisition: before 13 search retunes and never back, after none and back. (3) Identifier table, neighbours and secondary control channels are dropped when the control channel turns out to be another site or system (stale identifiers placed the new site's channels). (4) IDEN_UP_TDMA channel types 0 to 2 are plain carriers, not unplaced. (5) The receiver's call log placed Phase 2 grants without frequency (no IDEN_UP_TDMA). (6) App: the 96-message history was replayed as fresh grants whenever Follow was turned on; a hopped Phase 1 call that the voice channel shows as encrypted is left at once. Not fixable here: the Phase 2 SACCH/FACCH layout is still OnAir's own (needs a recording), DMR Tier III / Capacity+ / Connect+, NXDN and TETRA have no control-channel follower (DmrModel exists but is not wired), CQPSK simulcast.

2026-10-27 (fix33-trunking, second round): (a) Phase 2 SACCH/FACCH now decode with the coding of the standard (RS(63,35) shortened/punctured with an erasure decoder written here, CRC-12, 21 / 18 byte MAC PDUs, END_PTT fields at the PTT's offsets, MAC message length table); structure from OP25's public behaviour, verified against our own encoder only (no TIA text, no recording): needs a real Phase 2 channel. Old layout kept as fallback. (b) Phase 2 capture range 2.7 kHz to 6 kHz (spectrum coarse search; lock on an alias is dropped). Offset sweep: +4 kHz locked on the -1.9 kHz alias and found no calls; now +4, -5, +5, +6 kHz give 100 % of the voice frames. (c) Simulcast, Phase 1 FM-discriminator receiver (240 ksps, 25 dB, 12 s of control channel, second transmitter 3 dB down, 5 Hz away): C4FM 99 % at 0 to 40 µs delay, 82 % at 80 µs, 50 % with an equal-strength second transmitter at 40 µs; a synthetic CQPSK (pi/4 DQPSK, RRC 0.2, same phase step per dibit) 86 % with one transmitter, 74 / 52 / 26 / 14 / 1 % at 0 / 10 / 20 / 40 / 80 µs. The CQPSK model is a guess at LSM's pulse shape, not a recording, and a real LSM signal is built to suit a discriminator, so these numbers are a pessimistic bound; no bug was found, the missing piece would be a CQPSK demodulator with an equaliser (a feature, not done). Needs a recording from a simulcast LSM system.

## 2026-10-27: P25 voice radio, issue #349 (fix33-p25-2dev)
The P25 follower can use a second receiver for the voice channel while the first stays on the control channel. Core: `FollowConfig::voiceRadio`, actions `OpenRemote` / `CloseRemote`, `Follower::remoteFailed`, a start window (4 s to be heard) and a 3 s back-off after a call the radio never heard; grant repeats do not keep a voice-radio call open, only voice does. App (p25_ui.cpp): a "Voice radio" picker (Auto / Off / RX n) in the Follow tab; the follower belongs to one receiver, the voice radio is hopped with `Engine::setStandard` (no restart), tuned without saving its prefs, given the sound while it is on the call, and put back afterwards; P25 can now run on two receivers (receiverModeBusy). Verified: test_p25_trunk has a fake second receiver (hand-off to idle, preempt, hang time, Phase 2 slot, encrypted, failed retune, radio gone, never heard, single radio unchanged). Not verified: two real radios, the Phase 2 hop on a real voice channel, the sound switching.
## 2026-10-26: feat33-spectrum (Controls, fast waterfall, big spectrum FFTs; owner's three requests)
1. **View > Controls (keys and mouse) ...** (app/ui/input_map.h/.cpp, docs/CONTROLS.md, tests/app/test_input_map.cpp). Every key action the spectrum area had (Space start/stop, 1-9 modes, Ctrl+arrows dB range, Ctrl+Page Up/Down) goes through one table with a binding (key or middle/back/forward mouse button, with exact modifiers); new actions (tune up/down by the step, zoom in/out/whole band, pan, step larger/smaller, waterfall pause, mute) start unbound. The wheel has a table per modifier (none, Shift, Ctrl, Alt, Ctrl+Shift) for the spectrum and the waterfall: zoom both / frequency only / level-time only / tune by the step / pan / scroll the waterfall back in time / nothing, plus a tuning step (10 Hz to 1 MHz) and notches per step. Conflicts are marked, reserved keys (Esc, Ctrl+K, Ctrl+Shift+L, left/right buttons) refused, reset to defaults, saved in `ctlKeys`/`ctlWhSpec`/`ctlWhWf`/`ctlStep`/`ctlNotch` (and in Tools > Backup). Defaults are exactly the old behaviour. What the wheel did: plain wheel on the spectrum zooms the frequency AND the dB range (the "max, min" the owner disliked), Ctrl frequency only, Alt both; with Shift held the plots took no wheel at all (the Shift+drag signal selection set NoPan), so Shift and Ctrl+Shift were dead (the comment in plot.cpp said Shift = level only: it never worked). `plt::SetNextWheelAxes()` + `Flags_NoDrag` carry the choice into the plot; `wheelPolicy()` in plots.cpp does tune/pan/scroll. The web client has its own JS input code: not touched. Checked in a hidden run with scripted wheel events (DECT2_WHEELTEST=1): default table both axes / Ctrl frequency only, a custom table frequency-only zoom (dB range untouched), tune +3 x 25 kHz, pan.
2. **Fast waterfall** (CW). Limit before: one row per spectrum frame, 30 a second (the engine publishes one frame per 33 ms and the screen only ever takes the newest). `SpectrumAnalyzer::setFastRows()` now makes rows from its own windowed transforms of ~20 ms of signal (1024..65536 points; up to four per row; a budget of 8 M points a second) every 1/rate seconds, queued and taken in batches (`Engine::takeFastRows`); `Waterfall::fast()` (>= 28 rows/s), `offerFast()` writes every row (no averaging down the rows), the scale uses the exact row time, the pointer tooltip on the waterfall names the age of the row, a focused live fast waterfall draws at 60 fps. Waterfall popup: Fast (Morse / CW) 50/100/200/500 and a "CW / fast" button (100 rows/s). Checked with a generated 20 wpm Morse file at 96 ksps and 2.4 Msps in a hidden run: 100/200 rows a second made and taken, Morse dots 6 rows high and dashes 18 readable in the picture. Cost 0.1 % to 2 % of a core (bench_specfft). Tests: test_spectrum_fastrows (count, spacing, keyed-tone edges, level, bounded queue, frames untouched), test_wf_fast, test_frame_pace.
3. **Big FFTs.** Reproduced and profiled on the Mac (bench_specfft, bench_fft; table in docs/PERFORMANCE.md section 9): the transform is not the cost (262144 bins at 10 Msps: 4.4 % of a core with Accelerate, about 5-10 % with the portable kernels), but the frame rate was fs / N below ~4 Msps (131072 bins at 2.4 Msps: 18 frames/s, 262144: 9; at 1 Msps 7 and 3.7): that is the "lag". Fix: with the sample rate known, a size for which two blocks do not fit in a frame period is made from overlapping windows every fs / 35 samples: 29-30 frames/s everywhere (CPU 3.7 -> 19.5 ms per signal second at the worst case). Automatic sizes never overlap; small sizes bit for bit as before. Also: `feed()` no longer holds the analyser's mutex through the transforms, so the analysis (decoder) thread never waits for a 262144-point FFT (worker-private buffers, generation counter; ThreadSanitizer/ASan clean, test_spectrum_threads). Tried and dropped: a cache-blocked stage order in `fftSplit` (bit-exact but not faster on Apple silicon). `fftSplit` itself, the frames' scale and the decoders are untouched (fft_bits, spectrum_fastlog pass). Not done: a zoom-FFT / decimating to the visible span (changes what a big size means), GPU transforms (none exist in the app), sizes above 262144.

## 2026-10-26: feat33-wenet (Wenet receive in the SSDV mode)
Wenet (projecthorus/wenet, the Project Horus 115.2 kbaud image link) added as two speeds of the SSDV mode, not a new mode: same SSDV packets, other radio link, so no new family or poll slot (mode numbers unchanged). New: `core/include/dect2/ssdv/wenet.h`, `core/src/ssdv/wenet_ldpc.cpp` (CRC-16, repeat-accumulate encoder, layered min-sum decoder), `wenet_rx.cpp` (own wide front end: offset + AFC + 160 kHz low-pass + discriminator, two bit slicers with a transition clock loop, unique-word search with both polarities, 40 / 32 bit UW), `wenet_tables.cpp` (the 516 x 12 check table and the 125-byte scrambler, copied from the Wenet repo). 115.2 kbaud = serial-word framing (what the RFM98W / RFM22B UART transmitters send); 96 kbaud = raw scrambled (I2S transmitter). SsdvReceiver runs both next to the narrow chains (setBaud 115200 / 96000 pick one); type 0x55 payloads go through ssdv::check and the existing assembler, idle and telemetry types are counted. Generator: `wenet` and `slipEveryBits` in SsdvGenConfig. Tests: `tests/ssdv/test_ssdv_wenet.cpp` (reference LDPC vector, encoder parity, round trip, offsets, ppm, drift, noise, bit slips, sample rates, noise alone, wrong fixed speed). UI: two Wenet entries under Advanced > Speed, help text, credits row. Needs a user test with a real recording (no real Wenet signal was available). Not done: telemetry packet contents (GPS, CBOR), I2S baud rates under 96 kbaud.

## 2026-10-28: Internet TV (IPTV) mode (feat34-iptv, 3.4)
Owner: "tv streamed off the internet, can we add that please? a guy made a video saying OnAir doesn't do that". New mode row **Internet TV (IPTV)**, family 209 (engine standard 211, no receiver and no poll slot; docs/MODE-NUMBERS.md), user page docs/IPTV.md. Design decision: the bundled static FFmpeg is built `--disable-network --disable-everything` (file protocol only, LGPL, no TLS library), so I did not turn FFmpeg's network on; OnAir fetches the bytes itself (core/src/tv/iptv_http.cpp HTTP/1.1 client, iptv_stream.cpp HLS / MPEG-TS-over-HTTP / UDP / RTP reader with reconnect, iptv_parse.cpp M3U, XMLTV and HLS playlist parsers, iptv_aes.h AES-128-CBC for plain HLS encryption) and hands MPEG-TS packets to the existing Player through the external-playback path of the recordings, so decoding, subtitles, audio tracks, full screen and recording are the antenna player's. https: NSURLSession on macOS (tested against example.com and expired.badssl.com: valid, bad-certificate reason, Advanced override), WinHTTP on Windows (written from the docs, not run), the system OpenSSL 3 on Linux when found at configure time (tls_conn.cpp got an `insecure` flag, off unless the user ticks it). DRM (SAMPLE-AES / FairPlay keyformat / #KODIPROP licence) is refused with a plain message. Not in this version: RTSP, DASH, fMP4/CMAF HLS, separate audio renditions, .gz guides, onair:// and drag and drop. Tests: tests/tv/test_iptv.cpp (ctest `iptv`): URL resolution, M3U variants (BOM, CR/LF/CR, quoting, Kodi pipe options, EXTGRP either side, duplicates, 50,000 channels in about 40 ms), XMLTV (zones, CDATA, entities, 30,000 programmes in 20 ms; the first version was quadratic, 21 s), HLS parsing, FIPS-197 AES vector, TS alignment, RTP, and the reader against a local server serving a self-made HLS stream (live that grows, bitrate switch, VOD end, AES-128, 403/404/refused/HTML/RTSP messages, dropped connections, redirects, chunked bodies, UDP).
GUI check (hidden renamed copy, fresh DECT2_DATA_DIR, self-made 40 s HLS from ffmpeg testsrc2 on a local python server, a 3-channel M3U): the Internet TV tab shows the channel list with groups, plays through the antenna player (h264 640x360 + AAC, 99 frames decoded, A/V -3 ms, no errors) and the status line shows kind, resolution, codecs, download speed and buffered seconds. Found and fixed on the way: the stream thread outlived the engine at exit (crash in the teletext feed), so `iptvShutdown()` now stops it first.

## 2026-10-30: nRF24L01 receive mode (3.4, community request)

- New mode "nRF24L01" (family 210, engine standard 212, poll slot 186; row in docs/MODE-NUMBERS.md): Enhanced ShockBurst sniffer, GFSK 250 kbit/s / 1 / 2 Mbit/s, channel N = 2400 + N MHz, 3-5 byte addresses found by trying positions and lengths and accepting a valid CRC-16; CRC-8 frames need a third sight. core/src/nrf24 (proto, rx, gen), app/nrf24 panel (Frames / Addresses / Other bursts), docs/nrf24.md, tests nrf24_proto / rx / sweep / engine (+ app/test_nrf24_mode wiring test, built with the app).
- Receiver: one 450 kHz detector on the raw IQ, the three demodulators run on the stored burst only (about 20-35% of one core at 10 Msps). Lessons: detecting on the whole 8 Msps band loses 250 kbit/s frames (in-band SNR); noise-only needs the power averaged over a few independent filtered samples; an end margin that reaches into noise ruins the level estimate; CRC-8 chance matches recur with every repeat of a real frame, so they need a stricter rule (third sight, never on an address known to use CRC-16, dropped when a CRC-16 frame explains the burst).
- Yield on the synthetic signal (about 300 frames/s offered): 250k full at 14 dB Eb/N0, 1M and 2M full at 18 dB (92% / 90% at 16 dB); short 2 Mbit/s frames (3-byte address, 4 byte payload) are the weakest. Carrier offset +-100 kHz and +-1000 ppm clock cost nothing. Needs a user test: no real recording, no on-air check. No GUI screenshot taken (app not built in this worktree).

## 2026-10-30: decoder quality campaign (feat34-quality)
Full GitHub sweep (sens_sweep, full tier, 8 trials) of every mode, table in docs/SENSITIVITY.md. Three of the largest gaps were the harness, not receivers: the cubic clock-error interpolator (ATSC and wide OFDM looked unable to follow 50 ppm), the audio tone measured at a fixed 1000 Hz (FM sro50ppm +7 dB), and a 50 Hz phase noise linewidth for TV tuners; fixed in tools/sens. Real fixes: TETRAPOL frequency control (cfo20ppm threshold 17 -> 10 dB, test fails before / passes after), ATV (echo 9 -> 7 dB; line and field sync hold through noise: rows from counted lines, in-gate pulses of any width, field sync run tolerates a lost broad pulse, matched filters after a missed field sync; fields lost at 8 dB with 50 ppm 53-61 of 65 -> 64-67 of 65). Not changed on purpose: FM offcentre / rician (the live engine switches the station finder off by design), Z-Wave offcentre (outside the channel), narrowband TU6 (fading). Two of the five planned fixes were found to be harness artefacts, so only two receiver fixes are in; the table lists the next candidates.

## 2026-10-31: chosen rate and Android stream freeze (fix35-rate-android)
Rate (Discord, "10 Msps shows 5"): read every place the chosen rate goes to a driver. App: applyBandwidth keeps the saved rate in tune.sampleRate with rateChosen set; the HackRF drivers (own USB and libhackrf) send tune.sampleRate as it is, no cap, no halving. Engine: radioTune gives the radio the same rate except offset tuning (modes 0-15 with auto offset, the radio goes faster, never slower) and "Decimate in OnAir". What is half of the rate is the HackRF's automatic baseband filter (5 MHz at 10 Msps, hackrfAutoFilterHz), which is what a user sees as a 5 MHz-wide capture; feat35-lane-a makes that filter selectable, so no HackRF file was changed here beyond the test hook in makeSource. New: the UI "radio runs at ..." now compares the rate the driver confirmed (radioRate), not the post-resampler rate, and says why; start logs "sample rate: asked / set / confirmed". Test tests/engine/test_chosen_rate_reaches_radio.cpp: a fake HackRF (setTestSourceFactory) records the rate it is given for 12 modes x 7 rates, with and without auto offset, plus retune and a driver that caps. Not reproduced on a real radio (no hardware here).
Android freeze (Vivo X300 Ultra, Android 17): read UsbFdSys/rtl_fd_shim readAsync: an EPIPE ("broken pipe") URB status was retried 8 times without CLEAR_HALT, then the loop returned and the radio was reopened, but a halted endpoint stays halted, so the next stream failed the same way; a stream that simply stopped completing was only noticed after 10 s. Both fixed with StreamWatchdog (core/include/dect2/source/stream_watchdog.h): 2 s stall or a burst of 3 failed transfers climbs clear-halt, clear-halt, USB reset, reopen. Android also has no stderr, so every driver message ("radio lost", "reconnected") was invisible: diagLog() now goes to the engine log through setDiagSink. engine_watch: an Android Native USB radio silent for 8 s is stopped and started again like a HackRF. Verbose logging: View > Verbose logging (desktop, also in the Log panel with Save log), Settings > Verbose logging and a "verbose" chip in the Log screen (Android, share button already there); logs USB MB/s, transfers, errors, stalls, restarts, resets and UI frame times. Tests: test_stream_watchdog (ladder, with a made-up clock), test_usb_fd (fake usbfs: halted endpoint, stuck endpoint, 2.3 s stall). The Vivo cause itself was not reproduced (no such phone); the fix covers the failure the log points to.

2026-10-31 (lane w35-a): Discord "Add 10500bd AERO inmarsat" (only 600 and 1200 bit/s carriers visible). The 10500 bit/s OQPSK demodulator, frame format and rate detection already existed (aero_demod / aero_phy / aero_rx); the cause was in carrier selection. The Aero receiver kept at most 6 channels and took new carriers in order of peak level, but a 10500 bit/s carrier spreads its power over ~10 kHz and reads about 8 dB lower per bin than a 600/1200 bit/s carrier, so on a busy band the narrow carriers (and noise ghosts) filled all six slots first. Fixes: 12 channels, wide regions (>= 6 kHz) get an 8 dB ordering bonus and may replace the weakest channel that holds no sync; the Inmarsat all-carriers manager gives wide carriers the same ordering bonus when the CPU budget parks windows; carrier rows carry a width based rate guess (`~10500`, `~600`) until a decoder holds sync, and the Carriers view prints a per-rate count of decoded P channels. Tests: tests/aero/test_aero_crowded.cpp (10500 bit/s channel among 10 to 14 strong carriers, cfo +4.3 / -3.1 kHz, Eb/N0 6 to 12 dB, 2.4 and 4 Msps; fails 3 of 3 on the old code), tests/inmsat/test_inmsat_detect.cpp case 6 (weak 10500 bit/s found and classed wide at Eb/N0 4 to 12 dB, with offset, no ghost on noise) and 7 (rate guess). Existing aero_*, inmsat_* tests pass. Synthetic only: no real 10500 bit/s capture; the App change (inmsat_ui.cpp) was not compiled here (app build off).

## 2026-10-31: Inmarsat on Airspy (lane w35-a, Discord "AIRSPY on Inmarsat all carriers not working")
Root cause found by a sample-rate sweep through the manager (tests/inmsat/test_inmsat_rates.cpp): the Inmarsat window channelizer decimates by powers of two, so an Airspy R2 at 10 Msps (and 2.5 Msps, and an RTL at 2.4) gives each Aero window a 600-625 ksps input of which only about 210 kHz is pass-band; the Aero band search took its noise floor as the median of the whole input, which was then the empty stop-band, so the threshold was far too low and no carrier was ever found (0 of 3 carriers at 10, 2.5 and 2.4 Msps; 3 and 6 Msps worked because their windows run at 375 ksps). Fix: `AeroReceiver::setSearchLimit` (default off, so the stand-alone Aero mode is unchanged) and the manager limits each window's search to its pass-band. All of 2.4, 2.5, 3, 6, 10 Msps now lock all three carriers, also with a DC spike, a -40 dB IQ image and a carrier offset; Inmarsat-C locks at 2.5, 6 and 10 Msps. Driver side read (source_airspy.cpp: float32 IQ, rate pick, bias-T off) found nothing wrong. Honest gap: synthetic only; the 6 Msps Mini case already passed before the fix, so if a Mini still fails on a real capture the cause is something this sweep cannot show (real spectrum shape, gain).

## 2026-10-31: HackRF baseband filter choice (lane w35-a, Discord "hack rf: rate/filter")
The driver already derived the analogue filter from the rate (Auto: half the rate, 5 MHz at 10 Msps; wider for a wide channel), and the app already drops a mode's narrow filter (1.75 MHz of the 2 Msps modes) when the user raises the rate. What was missing was a way to choose it. HackRF One and HackRF Pro (USB driver, libhackrf fallback and the settings listing) now offer Advanced > Baseband filter: Auto (default) + the 16 MAX2837 widths 1.75 ... 28 MHz; a choice beats the mode's filter and Auto, no clamp against the rate. hackrfFilterFor()/hackrfFilterSetting() in hackrf_usb.cpp; checks in tests/source/test_native.cpp (all 16 widths, Auto, junk value). Not tried on a real HackRF; the 4 unrelated FAILs of test_native (RTL tone, V4, HF+) are on this Mac's fake library.

## 2026-10-31: Smoother sonde landing prediction (feat35-lane-b)
Discord request "more precise sonde prediction". The old `predictSondeLanding` (core/include/dect2/sonde/sonde_land.h) used the last frame's ground speed and heading, so a spiralling parachute moved the cross by kilometres per frame. Added `predictSondeLandingTrack`: drift and vertical speed are the least squares slope of position and altitude against time over the last N seconds of the track (default 10, Advanced slider "landing average" 0 to 60 s in the radiosonde panel, pref `sondeAvgS`; 0 = old behaviour), and for the layers (500 m) still below the sonde the wind from the ascent track is blended in (60 %). Without a track (only the 5 newest sondes carry one) it falls back to the instantaneous method. Test tests/sonde/test_sonde_land_smooth.cpp: synthetic sheared wind, 31 m/s spiral, 3 m GPS noise, 8.3 s period: rms error to the true landing 21.4 km instantaneous, 3.5 km with the 10 s fit, 1.4 km with ascent layers. Not checked on a real flight recording; the layer blend weight is a heuristic and the fall-speed model is unchanged.

Discord: Inmarsat-C and Aero messages could not be exported. ## 2026-10-31: RDS block voting and LoRa lost-sample recovery (feat35-lane-f)
#15: grep first, RDS had burst and two-symbol soft correction plus a repeat check on corrected groups, but nothing combined bad blocks across copies. `RdsDecoder::voteBlock` keeps the last 8 bad blocks per position with bit reliabilities; three close copies (within 8 bits, under ~3.5 s) vote bit by bit and the result is used only if the checkword fits and every copy is within 5 bits; the group-level repeat rule still applies. `setVoting()` switches it (on with the top correction level). Test `fm_rds` (BER 3, 5, 8 percent, soft errors): +2, +8, +18 percent groups, less wrong text than without voting, no vote from 2M noise bits. Untested: real weak-signal FM captures (simulated bit errors only).
#22 (#386): recoverJumps already existed but only searched multiples of 11.7 chips (96 samples at 250 kHz/2.048 Msps); a synthetic drop of any other sample count failed (6 of 48 recovered). It now falls back to every shift modulo 2^SF with up to two jumps and a bigger work budget (same parity + CRC + single-candidate rules). Test `mesh_phy`: 46 of 48 synthetic drops recovered (1-2 drops, 20..333 samples, SF8..10, 125..500 kHz), 0 false payloads on 180 damaged frames, slowest search 30 ms. Not done: a drop inside the 8 header symbols or the preamble; two drops at SF10/500k; real RTL-SDR data.

New: `core/src/geo/wx_overlay.cpp` geolocates every pixel of a Meteor-M swath from SGP4 and the picture start time (sub-point + perpendicular spherical offset), resamples it into a Mercator or plate-carree raster with triangle filling (no seams, date line unwrapped), projects a GOES full disk from scan angles, and writes GeoTIFF (EPSG:4326, RGBA) and KML. `app/map/wx_overlay.cpp` draws it on View > Map with an opacity slider, Export button and Advanced knobs; meteor_ui and goes_ui feed it only while the map is open. Untested on real signals: pixel-x direction, real swath width, the picture-start time, and GOES disk size in HRIT full-disk pictures.

## 2026-10-31: P25 control channel recovery and DMR cold-start resync (feat35-lane-g)
Before building: grep showed #348 was already mostly fixed (own-site candidates only, 12 s dwell, no retune onto the current frequency, 227f3e147) and a short-call harness existed (tests/dmr/test_dmr_short.cpp). P25: the follower now remembers the frequencies a control message was really heard on (last four) and hunts those first, most recent first, then the system's candidates; a first hunt step on the channel the radio is already on waits one more loss time, not a whole dwell (test: 40 s drop, then the site moves its control channel to its secondary; never visits a channel outside the site; re-lock and move-find times). DMR: the coarse carrier search accepted nothing before 8 spectrum frames and then waited for two agreeing estimates, so a cold receiver with an offset above the sync tracker's reach (about 7 kHz) lost the first short call. A carrier 20x above the noise is now accepted after 3 frames (64 ms), and the last 0.3 s of the signal is replayed through the corrected oscillator (Link::rewind keeps the clock exact) so the sync that already went by is found. New tests/dmr/test_dmr_resync.cpp: cold first call, offsets up to 9.5 kHz, 8 to 25 dB, clock error, mobile and direct; before: +9 kHz 0 to 38 %, after: 100 %, -7 kHz in test_dmr_short 88 % to 100 %. Untested on real signals; offsets beyond 10 kHz are still treated as the neighbouring channel on purpose.

## 2026-10-31: Wide multi-signal decode (feat35-lane-g)
Before building: grep showed Multi-VFO (3.4, `VfoBank`: NFM/AM/SSB/WFM plus pager and APRS channels the user places by hand), `WideModes` (one mode's carriers over the whole span, per-bank CPU budget), `MultiTv` (DVB-T/T2 or satellite slots of one standard) and `autodet` (voice and TV identifier). Nothing joined them, so a capture with a DMR carrier, a pager channel and two TV standards needed one mode at a time. Built `WideSession` (core/include/dect2/engine/wide_session.h) on top of all four, no duplicate decoders: CarrierFinder finds carriers (TV zones excluded through the new `CarrierFinder::setExclusions`), each is watched 1.2 s and classified (`classifyVoice`; else the known channel list or frequency database for pagers/APRS; else air band AM), TV slots are found with SpanScanner and identified with `detectTv`. Each carrier goes to the decoder of what it is: `WideModes::setFollowOnlyFor` (new, per-mode follow list), `VfoBank` for AM/NFM, `MultiTv::setAllowedSlots` (new) for DVB-T and DVB-T2 side by side. List with enable/disable, pin, priority, manual kind; one CPU guard over all decoders drops the lowest ranked unpinned entry (never the listened one), shows why, and restores when the load stays well under budget. Tests: `wide_session` (2.4 Msps, DMR + P25 + pager + APRS + AM, all off-raster; switch off/on; guard drop/restore; sound of the kept AM station) and `wide_session_tv` (20 Msps, DVB-T2 + DVB-T + DMR + pager). Not yet verified on real signals; see docs/WIDE-SESSION.md.

## 2026-10-31: Weak-signal helpers: voice noise remover everywhere, diversity pair (feat35-lane-g)
Before building: grep showed RNNoise (`NeuralNr`) only in the General receiver (NFM/WFM/AM) and Airband, while every other voice mode (DMR, P25, NXDN, D-STAR, dPMR, TETRA, M17, SSB ...) writes through `AudioOut` into the shared `AudioCleanup` chain that had only the spectral reducer; nothing existed for two-radio combining (only the wide stitching of `synth_group`, which puts radios side by side, not on one frequency). So the neural stage went into the shared chain (per-channel `NeuralNr` made in `prepare()`, switch and strength per profile, one click in the simple view for speech kinds) instead of editing each mode, which covers every voice mode with one toggle and no change to the others when it is off. The diversity pair is `dsp/diversity.{h,cpp}` (STFT per-bin maximal-ratio and selection combiner: noise from the median bin, branch gain, cross-spectrum phase loop for the oscillator offset and drift, delay from the cross-correlation, plus `diversityCoarseAlign` for the tens of milliseconds the radios start apart) and `source/diversity_source.{h,cpp}` (a `SynthGroupDef` with `diversity` set, saved as an optional sixth field so old strings still load, listed as "Diversity: name", falls back to one radio when the other fails), with a one-click button in the Group radios window. Tests with two noisy synthetic copies: equal radios gain 2.8 of 3.0 dB, unequal 0.7 of 1.0, three seeds, any chunking, a dead antenna, independent noise (must not lock), the whole pair with fake radios started 18 ms and 2.1 kHz apart (about +3 dB). Not tried on real dongles; weak branches (below about -3 dB per bin) lose 1 to 2 dB against the ideal. Docs: `docs/DIVERSITY.md`, `docs/noise-remover.md`.
## 2026-10-31: P25 follower fixes (fix35-p25)
Merged feat35-lane-g (control channel recovery, #348) first, then fixed the follower that "detects but follows nothing". Single-radio hop now gets a start grace (hopStartSec 6 s, the retune restarts the receiver) instead of 2 s, so it no longer closes before any voice and loops hop/return; a hop that heard nothing backs the same talkgroup off for 8 s. The hopped radio reports the terminator (back to the control channel at once after a TDU). Late grant updates for a call that just ended are ignored (6 s). Emergency grants rank first. A grant heard before its identifier row is retried on the repeat, not dropped. Unplaceable TDMA channel types give an honest reason. Phase 2 grants wait for NET_STS (WACN / system) instead of hopping with a zero seed. Control channel hunt ignores a different NAC. Phase 1 calls inside the capture live on their wide-bank carrier (voice and terminator from the carrier), not on grant repeats. Sound picks the nearest carrier. Telemetry TSBK ring 96 -> 512 with a visible gap counter. Follow is on by default, retune on, saved in prefs, hops no longer save the voice channel as the user's frequency, and a one-line status ("Following TG 1234 on 851.2375 MHz, slot 2" / "Not following TG n: reason") shows in Live and Follow. Not done: Phase 2 MAC opcode length table and ESS robustness (lengths not verifiable without TIA-102.BBAC), independent Phase 2 generator, MBT trunking messages. The 10 Msps -> 5 Msps path is pub/fix35-rate-android's.

## 2026-10-31: Synthetic P25 trunking harness (fix35-p25)
tests/p25/p25_scenario.h: a deterministic world of Phase 1 C4FM and Phase 2 H-DQPSK transmitters at absolute frequencies (control channel scripts with IDEN_UP / VU / TDMA band plans for 700 / 800 MHz and VHF / UHF, NET / RFSS / adjacent status, group grants; voice channels with HDU / LDU1 / LDU2 / TDU, placeholder voice frames; scrambled Phase 2 with ESS), impairments (carrier offset, symbol clock error, noise, two-ray fading, DC spur, tuner ppm), a fake retunable radio (0.3 s settle after a retune) and a Rig that runs the real P25Receiver / P25p2Receiver / WideModes / trunk::Controller the way app/p25/p25_ui.cpp does. Four ctests use it: p25_scenario_cc (CC detection, identifier maths, grant frequencies), p25_scenario_hop (hop, voice in the log, terminator, hang, dead grant back-off, encrypted voice channel, CC loss and regain), p25_scenario_slice (wide capture, Phase 1 slices from the bank, Phase 2 as the second receiver, slice limit), p25_scenario_p2 (Phase 1 vs Phase 2 by identifier, scrambled Phase 2 hop, encrypted Phase 2). Rig mirrors the UI loop but is not the UI: keep the two in step. Not covered: real transmitters, real audio, Phase 2 symbol clock error, MAC opcode lengths.

## 2026-10-31: P25 audit fixes, second pass (fix35-p25)
Phase 2 receiver: a lock no longer reads the first bursts on a guessed slot (slotKnown: bursts are dropped until an I-ISCH names the slot, at most two, so a join mid-superframe cannot put a call on the wrong slot or descramble with the wrong sequence). The PTT's key ID and message indicator are read and kept per call; an encrypted call is logged once with algorithm, key and MI and its voice is skipped. A lost ESS (partial or uncorrectable) no longer makes a known call unknown (the algorithm and key of a call do not change; OP25 does the same); a call with no PTT and no ESS for 54 voice frames (three superframes) is labelled "encryption unknown" and skipped; held voice keeps the newest frames. MAC message lengths follow OP25's p25p2_tdma.cc rules (null information, avoid-zero-bias, 0x11 / 0x12 lists, manufacturer length byte, table), and the extended group grants 0xC0 / 0xC3 are read (field order unverified against the paywalled standard, lengths from the OP25 table). Phase 1 trunking: the explicit group update (opcode 0x03) follows channel (T), not channel (R) (OP25 tk_p25.py uses the first channel field). New: Controller::conversations() (calls, followed, encrypted and airtime per talkgroup) in the Talkgroups table. Tests: test_p25p2_audit, test_p25_audit. Third-party: only rules and field offsets taken from OP25 (GPL-3, same licence); no code copied. Not done: D21 (both slots followed), D22, M12, M14-M16, MBT trunking messages, A3-A7 timing, L17-L24 (their definitions were in an audit file that does not exist in this session).

## 2026-10-31: SSB conversations (feat35-ssb-conversations)
Owner idea: find SSB / USB / LSB conversations on the user's spectrum and organise them to listen to. Before building: grep showed the wide-capture Multi-VFO (listen without retuning), the signal identifier, the band plan, the transcription engine, the scanner and the automation events, but nothing that finds voice; the Shortwave mode branch (feat35-shortwave) is not on integ-35, so the panel is self-contained (View > Conversations) and the "Listen" hook is a Multi-VFO row.
`core/src/ssbconv`: Welch spectrum with a decayed max-hold, candidates 1.2-4.4 kHz wide, sideband from the spectral tilt (band convention only a weak vote), CIC3 + FIR channel, voice analysis (active-frame depth, 2.5-8 Hz modulation, pitch from the complex autocorrelation, tone and band checks, mirrored-sideband gate), tracks that follow drift, conversation book (overs split by pause or pitch change, voices, QSO / rag chew / net, S-units and trend, ACTIVE now, filters, CSV / JSON, timeline, favourites, ignore), auto-listen with hold / skip / fade, sweep plan for narrow radios, WAV + JSON clips, callsign reader. `app/ssbconv`: the window, tap and worker, Listen through a Multi-VFO row, events `signal.ssb_conversation_new/ended`, Demo (`--ssb-conversations demo`).
Lessons from the synthetic tests: a 120 Hz threshold on single bins misses real speech (the average SNR is spread over 2.4 kHz; smooth over 250 Hz and hold the shape); fricatives made of noise were two-sided and wrecked the first scenes; the sideband must be judged on the held shape, not on one window (formant gaps flipped it and made ghost tracks); the same station must keep its conversation when its frequency estimate is corrected (match by track); keyed data (ALE bursts) has the depth of speech over its pauses, so depth is taken among the active frames only; pitch picked half the period until peaks within 7% of the best were preferred; the pause rule alone merged two stations that alternate within 1 s, a three-window pitch change splits them.
Screenshots (renamed binary, --hidden --mute --shot, Demo scene, no duplicate-ID report): list, timeline, filter, settings in shots/ of the worktree. On GitHub the first CI run compiled everything of this feature on macOS, Linux and Windows (MinGW) and only failed at the link of dect2 on integ-35's missing doctor_ui.cpp (fixed upstream, merged here).
Gaps: no real HF recording; speech is synthetic; the transcription engine is not connected (callsign reader is a hook); the `sigid` veto is wired in the window but was not run on a signal (a fake in the tests); the retune path for conversations outside the span and the band scanner are not tested against a radio.

Review fix (2026-10-31): two SSB stations 3.3-4 kHz apart that talked at the same time lost the second one (its neighbour's edge sat on the mirrored side of its carrier and spoiled the frame energy, pitch and band share). The scanner now filters each channel to the wanted sideband (150-3150 Hz) before the voice analysis; the carrier/tone check and a new mirrored-sideband check (same audio at -800..-250 and +250..+800 Hz = AM/DSB) use the unfiltered window. New test testNeighboursAndAm (adjacent stations, 3 kHz wide AM with and without carrier). Measured: 2.048 Msps capture 8% of one core, 10 Msps 33% (worker thread, not the radio thread).

Kept every list (combo, segmented bar, Mode menu) and added a "Modes" button beside the mode name in the top bars (new and classic interface), the Mode menu item "Browse all modes..." and the M key. It opens a large modal window with every mode as a button grouped by family (tabs All / Favourites / Recent / Compatible with my radio, tag chips, a family rail, search, keyboard moves, a preview card). Logic is header-only and unit-tested (`app/ui/mode_picker_logic.h`, `tests/app/test_mode_picker.cpp`); tags, needs and minimum rate are derived from the group and `modeTuning()` in `app/ui/mode_info.cpp`, no rows edited. A radio that cannot reach a mode only greys the tile with a reason, selecting is never blocked. `gModeSignalHint` is the hook for the identifier's "Signal detected" badge. Changes from the spec: the close X is the window's own title-bar X (no second button); Recent is an ordered list (`modeRecent` pref) instead of `parseList`, which sorts; the band for the fit check is the mode's default frequency, because `modeTuning()` min/max are opened to any frequency. See docs/MODE-PICKER.md.

## 2026-10-31: OnAir Web setup window (feat35-webshare)
Owner report: the two OnAir Web steps freeze, step 2 fails almost always, and there is no control (radio, range, decoders). Found by reading the code and the worker (docs/ONAIR-WEB-SHARING.md lists them all): the UI thread joined workers and slept, the server was started without `web_trust_proxy=1` (the WebSocket's https Origin was refused through the tunnel, every listener was 127.0.0.1), the settings file started no receiver (no `autostart`/`device`/`freq_mhz`/`rate_msps`) and left the API port 8765 at its default, step 2 restarted the server and radio for every change, never read the result ("Listed" whatever happened), needed OpenSSL (absent in the Windows packages), blocked on `getaddrinfo` without a limit, and retried a 422 "address not in DNS yet" only after 30-60 s. Built: `Server > Share on web` window (radio dropdown with range and rate, presets and whole span, centre/span/rate/gain, decoder checklist with CPU cost, codec and out-of-range marks and the legal-notice lock, limits, audio only, password, link mode, step 2 with exact preview, Test connection, status with link, QR, listeners, kbit/s, Apply changes live or by restart), saved in `settings.conf`; the listing is its own program (`onair-server --directory-run/--directory-preview/--directory-test`) that writes `web_directory_status.json` with the directory's answer turned into what failed and what to do; every request goes through the system curl with a hard limit and cancel; new `web_spectrum=0` (audio only); `tools/fake_directory.py` (same validation as the Worker, failure modes) and `tests/webshare/test_fake_directory.py`; `tests/webshare/test_webshare_core.cpp`. Not done: gain "auto" is the server default (no AGC in the server), no live change of radio/range without a restart, QR checked only by the unit tests (format bits, Reed-Solomon) until a decoder is run on a screenshot.

## 2026-10-31: Shortwave mode (feat35-shortwave)
Owner request: a shortwave mode that is the general receiver but lets the user press any HF decoder without leaving the window or the frequency (listening at 11 MHz in AM, go to 10 MHz where there is RTTY, press RTTY, it decodes). Built as a new mode (built as family 211, engine standard 213, slot 187, HF UTILITY group; renumbered at merge, see below): `ShortwaveReceiver` owns a `GeneralReceiver` for the ear and feeds the existing receivers (HF digital RTTY / SSTV / FT8 / HamDRM / keyboard modes, CW, PSK, ALE, JS8, ARDOP, PACTOR, MT63, FSQ, IFKP, NAVTEX / weather fax, time signals, HF waveform ID) the same capture with a dial offset from the pure `sw::Router`; the decoders follow retunes, run in parallel and keep their text; a lower sideband decoder gets the mirrored capture; RTTY baud / shift / polarity are automatic through the whole-span RTTY bank. The screen reuses the General receiver's code, adds band buttons with a remembered frequency per band and a kHz box, a decoder strip with Auto / None / Advanced, one pane per decoder with Clear / Save / Log. The General / Auto identifier hook is `decoderForSignalId` + `setDetected` (lane-b untouched). Tests: routing without a GUI and the real decoders on synthetic IQ with offsets and noise. docs/SHORTWAVE-MODE.md.

Four mode-list entries (families 232 - 235, engine standards 234 - 237, poll slots 232 - 235) on one receiver, `FygeoReceiver` (`core/src/fygeo/`). FY-4: the DVB-S2 receiver of the DVB-S/S2 mode with the link's symbol rate (90 / 120 / 1000 ksym/s; captures under 2 Msps are interpolated by a whole factor because that receiver refuses them), transport stream packets searched for CADUs on any PID (ASM in the first 128 bytes of a payload), VCDUs through the GOES xRIT layers (`Assembler::setRawFiles`: packet zone 1012, no CRC check, whole files out; `Mux` takes a zone size), FY-4 header dialect (type 1 image information with segment numbers, navigation, key; encrypted files kept and counted), JPEG 2000 segments decoded with a vendored OpenJPEG 2.5.3 (`third_party/openjpeg`, libopenjp2 only) and put into one picture per satellite, channel, region and time. FY-2: matched filter, squared-signal carrier search, Gardner, Costas, NRZ-M, a free-running PN follower for the 10000 bit preamble (frame sync works to a channel bit error ratio of about 25 %), BER from the preamble, descrambling, IR1 - IR4 and VIS (half resolution) lines into five pictures. ## 2026-11-01 Inmarsat Aero C channel voice (feat36-inmvoice)
Owner rule: no "can't do", study the reference. Read JAERO (MIT, github.com/jontio/JAERO; the task said GPL-3, its LICENSE is MIT) and libaeroambe. The C channel is 8400 bit/s OQPSK, root raised cosine 0.6; frame = two 52-symbol unique words (one per arm, JAERO's 216866263330005 / 3012071630031408) + 4096 coded bits; 16 interleaver blocks of 64x4; rate 2/3 puncturing (every 4th coded bit dropped); K=7 code 109/79; P channel scrambler restarted per frame; 2714 bits = 24 x (1 lead + 96 voice + 12 signalling) + 1+96+1, i.e. 25 AMBE frames of 12 bytes and 3 signal units (0x01 fill-in, 0x30 call progress with AES and GES, 0x60 acknowledge). New: aero_cchan.* (coding, framer with arm/polarity/shift handling and flywheel), aero_crx.* (one carrier, mixer + CIC + AeroDemod 8400 + framer; AeroDemod got setBitSink and alpha 0.6; Front moved to aero_front.h), aero_cgen.* (generator), manager C receivers per wide carrier (updateC, cost 0.5, 45 s give-up, 60 s back-off), VoiceMixer::pushCFrame + call list, Voice tab (aircraft, GES, Eb/N0, call history), Advanced switch. Codec: new id aeroambe (AMBE 4800x3600, 12 bytes as JAERO gives libaeroambe), plug-in source tools/addons/aeroambe (no vocoder; user builds with libaeroambe's mbelib). VERIFIED ON REAL DATA: JAERO's 8400bps_ambe_sample.ogg: 166 frames, 160 with CRC-good SUs, call-progress AES 7812E7 GES 90; the plug-in on the voice bytes gave 0 Golay/Hamming errors and speech bursts (29-33 s, 64-72 s) from level and zero-crossing statistics (not listened to). Tests: test_aero_cchan (coding, framer, chain sweeps: offsets +-2.6 kHz, +-1000 ppm, rates 48k..2.4M, chunk 1..65536, drift 5 Hz/s, level step, three calls on one receiver, no frames from noise or a P 10500 channel; decoder only 100% from 3 dB per channel bit vs theory 2.6; chain locks from 2.5 dB), test_aero_cchan_real (14 s excerpt in tests/data/aero, int8 48 kHz), test_inmsat_cvoice (two simultaneous calls in a 2.4 Msps capture through the manager and mixer with a fake codec). Note: at 24 kHz input the demod does not lock (squared-signal aliasing); the Front always hands it about 48 kHz so this does not matter. Not done: single-channel Aero mode still P only (voice lives in Inmarsat all carriers); C-channel assignment SUs from the P channel are not yet used to prime receivers; encrypted calls cannot be told apart (no marker known).
## 2026-11-01 Simple / Advanced view per mode (feat36-simpleadv)
Owner dislikes the overall UI: every mode with its own screens now has a Simple | Advanced switch (two pills above the tabs). Simple = spectrum plus ONE Command view: the mode header, the summary line, the meters as coloured read-outs and the live data in panes side by side; Advanced = the tabs as before. Framework in app/ui/kit/mode_panel.{h,cpp} and mode_panel_logic.h (pure, tests/app/test_mode_panel.cpp): ModeUi gets optional `command` and `noSimple`; a mode with neither gets a generic view (its tab on the first view; kit::modeNav hides the pills and keeps the first item, and for a mode with a Map view shows Live and Map as two panes on a wide window, giving the user's Advanced choice back afterwards). `modepanel::panes()` is the layout helper (side by side, wrapping, stacked, one pure width function). Saved per mode as `uiLevel.<family>[.<sub>]`; the profile default is decided once at start (`setupUiLevelProfile`): an existing profile Advanced, a new one (no settings) Simple. HFDL (messages + ground stations, band and channels folded) and DMR (calls and slots + channel signal) have their own `command`; every other mode uses the generic view. Design note docs/SIMPLE-ADVANCED.md. Test aids: env DECT2_UILEVEL=simple|advanced, `UILEVEL=simple tools/ui_layout_check.sh`. Lesson: the first shots showed Advanced because the API token is made before the "fresh profile" check and the nested child made the layout checker report cut content, so the Command view is drawn in the tabs' own window.

## 2026-11-02: PACTOR-II, PACTOR-III, PACTOR-IV (physical layer) and Multitone framing (feat36-pactor)
Three new mode-list entries (families 237 / 238 / 239, engine standards 239 - 241, poll slots 197 - 199; kNumFamilies 240, kPollSeqSlots 200) on one receiver, `PactorHfReceiver` (`core/src/pactorhf/`), receive only. Searches (SCS PACTOR-3 / PACTOR-4 technical descriptions, ARRL, Wavecom, the Korean "Pactor Series Overview", sigidwiki, github, the p4dragon downloads): no open decoder and no reverse-engineering paper of the PACTOR-II / -III / -IV channel coding exists; the public descriptions give only constraint length 9 or 7, rate 1/2 and the puncturing rates, not the polynomials, puncturing patterns, interleaver, scrambler or header layout. docs/pactor.md says OnAir does not reverse engineer proprietary protocols, so the modes stop at the physical layer: carriers found by the symbol clock line (PACTOR-II pair 200 Hz apart, PACTOR-III on the 480 + 120 n Hz grid with the tuning error searched and the speed level from the carrier count, PACTOR-IV 1800 baud single carrier at 1500 Hz), common symbol clock with ppm drift, differential phase steps per carrier (D-BPSK / QPSK / 8PSK / 16PSK, block-wise frequency follow), repeating frame period and constant part found by a z-score over lags, PACTOR-IV constellation estimate by power-law lines. Owner's PACTOR-II recording (1 Nov, 8532.8 kHz): two carriers 200 Hz apart, 100.000 baud, a frame repeating every 362 symbols = 3.62 s (22 copies); neighbouring copies agree in about 60 % outside the constant part, so a changing scrambler or paired frames: not worked out. Symbols are right in perhaps 80 % there (poor channel). Tests: synthetic generator with the exact symbols (all PACTOR-II orders, PACTOR-III levels 2 - 6, PACTOR-IV orders 2/4/8/16; tuning +-20 Hz, clocks to +-300 ppm, noise to 12 dB in 2.5 kHz, fading, noise and a tone alone must not be found), the receiver through the front end at 250 ksps, the engine, the owner's recording (26 s excerpt in tests/data/pactorhf). Known limits: no equaliser (a second path turns PACTOR-IV QPSK into "QAM"), PACTOR-IV constellation needs about 15 dB, PACTOR-III level 1 equals PACTOR-II, two-path hurts PACTOR-II/III, the synthetic signals are not real PACTOR frames. Sens harness entries pactor2 / pactor3 / pactor4 (AWGN threshold about 3 dB SNR in 2.5 kHz for II and III; the combined scenario has no 90 % success). Multitone: the detector now measures framing from the bit stream (analyseFraming: doubled bits, repeating marker and spacing, word list, repeated halves) and the owner's recording (Multitone 27599.8 kHz FM audio) gives FFSK 292.8 / 585.6 Hz at 585.6 baud, 48-bit frames of a 24-bit marker 000000101101001001110110 plus a 24-bit word, 26 distinct words, first ten a byte sent twice; docs/multitone.md; no meaning is claimed (format not public). Credits row for the public descriptions added (THIRD_PARTY.md, in-app Credits). Fix: panel standard of the new modes is the active standard (238 + V).
## 2026-11-02 AppImage update, search-results CSV, selcall in whole-span pagers (feat36-ux2)
1) Linux: the release workflow now builds OnAir-<ver>-linux-<arch>.AppImage from the portable package (tools/package/make_appimage.sh, appimagetool 1.9.0 with --appimage-extract-and-run); release.sh lists it. The updater has InstallKind::LinuxAppImage (APPIMAGE variable or *.AppImage with the type-2 header), picks the asset for the architecture, checks GitHub's digest AND the release's SHA256SUMS (they must agree; SHA256SUMS fills in a missing digest), stages `<file>.new` next to the old file and swaps it after exit through the same apply script, no root. Tests: a fake release of local files (wrong SHA256SUMS, wrong digest-less sum, not-an-AppImage, success + swap). Not run: the real appimagetool in CI, an AppImage on a real Linux desktop. 2) #411: new "Export results CSV..." in the Frequencies panel saves the rows now shown (search + filters, airport rows included) in the Memory CSV format; 3 new strings in all 13 languages (unreviewed list). Issue parts A (airport rows for the IATA code ASM are listed first, by design) and B (VDES ASM 2 frequency) left alone, B needs the ITU channel table. 3) setPagerOptions gained the selcall flag: the whole-span pager carriers now decode CCIR/ZVEI/EEA/EIA/DZVEI like the audio pager (existing Pagers checkbox, no new control); test_pager_wide_selcall.

## 2026-11-02 Iridium voice finished (feat36-iridvoice)
Studied iridium-toolkit (README: 2-Clause BSD; THIRD_PARTY.md and in-app credits corrected from "unknown licence") and gr-iridium. The toolkit hands its AMBE decoder the 312 bit voice field as 39 bytes with the first bit received as the LEAST significant bit (bits_to_dfs.py); OnAir had sent MSB first, now the same as the toolkit (plug-in id `iridium`, 39 bytes, documented in CODECS.md). Silence frames (LCW 001111 / 100..0, stats-voc.py) are counted and not sent. Calls got a level meter, Mute and Solo (soloed calls mixed, Listen ignored while any solo; muted calls still decode and record) and a Record calls switch. New test_iridium_voicechain: known voice payloads through generator, demodulator and frame layer, bit-exact at +-8 kHz offset, +600 kHz off-centre, +-37.5 kHz Doppler at 350 Hz/s, +-50 ppm clock, 8 bit + DC; 90 % whole bursts at Es/N0 12 dB against 11.2 dB theory. Not verified: a real Iridium recording and the real FEC / interleaving inside the (user-installed) vocoder; GUI shot not taken (no full app build).
